Moves: g5f4, g5g4, g5h4, g5h5.

If K(g5) -> h4:

If K(g5) -> h5:

h7h6, g7g6, h7h5.
These moves don't break the pin. f5 is still pinned.
But they don't move the King, so they don't give White an immediate chance to check via the King's new position.

However, in this position, Black is completely lost. The strategy should be to prolong the game.
Moving the King to h5 (g5h5) gets it further away from the white king.
Moving the King to f4 (g5f4) brings it closer to White's kingside pawns.

If g5h5, the King is at h5. The pawn at f5 is no longer pinned.

A5 and H5 are on the same rank.

Row 4: ♕ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ♟ ♚ ⭘
a5=♕, b5=⭘, c5=⭘, d5=⭘, e5=⭘, f5=♟, g5=♚, h5=⭘.

If the King moves to h5, the board becomes:
a5=♕, b5=⭘, c5=⭘, d5=⭘, e5=⭘, f5=♟, g5=⭘, h5=♚.

Yes, they are both on rank 5.
But wait, is there anything between a5 and h5?
Yes, the pawn at f5 is still there!
The pawn at f5 is at (f, 5). The Queen is at (a, 5) and the King is at (h, 5).
So the pawn at f5 blocks the check.
So g5h5 is legal because the pawn at f5 stays at f5 and blocks the Queen's line of sight to h5.

Similarly, g5f4, g5g4, g5h4 move the King off rank 5, so they are also legal.

Which move is best?
If I move the King to h5 (g5h5), I'm still on the 5th rank, protected by the pawn at f5.
If I move the King to h4 (g5h4), I'm off the 5th rank.
If I move the King to g4 (g5g4), I'm off the 5th rank.
If I move the King to f4 (g5f4), I'm off the 5th rank.

White's most dangerous pieces are the Queen at a5 and Rook at d6.
The Rook at d6 controls the 6th rank.
If I move Kg5-f4, I am moving towards the center.
If I move Kg5-h5, I stay on the rank.
If I move Kg5-h4 or g4, I move towards the white king.

Let's evaluate the pawn moves.
h7h6, g7g6, h7h5.
Moving a pawn might provide a shield later or simply waste a move.
But in such a losing position, often the best move is to make the opponent work for the mate.

Looking at the legal moves again: g5h5, g5h4, g5g4, g5f4, h7h6, g7g6, h7h5.
Given the extreme material disadvantage, there's no "winning" move. The goal is to avoid immediate blunder.
Moving the King to h5 keeps the King sheltered by the pawn at f5.
Moving the King to h4 or g4 might allow White to check from different angles.

Let's try g5h5. It keeps the King away from the center and uses the pawn as a shield.
